L'Inter ha mostrato la migliore versione dopo la sconfitta con il Milan. La reazione, ma non solo, qualcosa è cambiato anche nell'atteggiamento della squadra di Chivu. Il tecnico ha toccato le corde giuste, ma ha anche sistemato l'assetto tattico.

"La sensazione che Cristian Chivu avesse preparato una nuova versione dell’Inter si è avuta prima del fischio d’inizio, alla consegna della formazione. Presente dall’inizio in 11 delle 14 partite giocate fin lì, Yann Bisseck figurava in panchina dopo essere stato il migliore in campo (con Zielinski) nella trasferta europea di Rotterdam, particolare che escludeva una bocciatura tecnica. Semplicemente, Bisseck è per distacco il difensore più veloce della rosa, l’uomo irrinunciabile quando sposti in avanti il baricentro della squadra e hai bisogno di una freccia per rincorrere l’avversario che ha evitato il fuorigioco ed è in fuga verso la tua porta. Una specie di assicurazione sulla vita. Se hai in mente di difendere più basso, la rapidità diventa una questione di riflessi più che di corsa, e lì un Akanji ben concentrato si fa preferire. Contro il Como, l’Inter ha così mantenuto un baricentro decisamente basso — nemmeno 49 metri dalla propria porta — mentre nella gara con la Fiorentina, spinta anche dalle circostanze, l’aveva alzato a 63. E quattordici metri di campo fanno tutta la differenza del mondo", analizza La Gazzetta dello Sport.

Nel segmento di nove partite (sei di campionato e tre di coppa) appena concluso, l’Inter complessivamente non ha certo fatto male, e intendiamoci, stiamo parlando degli standard di uno squadrone. Chivu ha detto che poteva andare meglio ma anche peggio, i numeri propendono per il secondo scenario. In campionato poteva fare meno di 15 punti su 18 disponibili (soltanto la Lazio ne ha raccolto uno in più, quello che la mantiene davanti), perché oltre al netto ko nel derby, la Fiorentina è stata scavalcata nel recupero e il Venezia è stato battuto su autogol al tramonto del match. In Champions pesa l’inattesa sconfitta dal Bruges, è vero, ma la settimana dopo lo Shakhtar si è trovato sullo 0-0 con 25 minuti da giocare in superiorità numerica, e ha finito per perdere: se immaginate uno sliding doors in negativo, quella sarebbe stata la fine stagionale del torneo nerazzurro, in ampio anticipo sul passato non solo glorioso (le due finali) ma anche modesto (il Bodo). La questione dei contratti in sospeso avrebbe preso il sopravvento — che fare di Calhanoglu, presto 33enne? — e addio. Questo per dire che l’Inter aveva forzato il proprio destino anche prima di abbassarsi contro il Como.

« La meilleure équipe d'Europe, c'est nous » : Marc Cucurella s'enflamme pour le Real Madrid !

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

En pleine confiance, Marc Cucurella a profité de son passage sur les ondes espagnoles pour désigner le Real Madrid comme la meilleure équipe du continent. Le latéral gauche estime en effet que la formation madrilène propose actuellement le meilleur football en Europe, balayant au passage les récentes critiques subies par le club.

Invité au micro de Juanma Castaño dans l'émission nocturne El Partidazo de COPE, diffusée sur la station Cadena COPE dans la nuit du 9 au 10 novembre 2026, Marc Cucurella a placé le club madrilène comme la meilleure équipe d'Europe. Le joueur estime que la formation espagnole propose actuellement le meilleur football, au-dessus de n'importe quel rival, alors même que le club était la cible de nombreuses critiques quelques semaines auparavant. Une nette montée en puissance. Il faut dire que le Real Madrid reste désormais sur une série impressionnante de huit victoires consécutives toutes compétitions confondues.

Dans ce même entretien, l'international espagnol a également pris la défense de son coéquipier Vinícius Júnior face aux critiques. D'ailleurs, Marc Cucurella a tenu à mettre en avant Arda Güler, qu'il considère comme le joueur madrilène le plus impressionnant cette saison.

Interrogé sur la concurrence européenne et plus particulièrement sur le statut du PSG, le défenseur a placé sans hésiter le Real Madrid devant le club français. Tout en reconnaissant la solidité du projet et la qualité évidente de l'effectif du PSG, il voit l'équipe madrilène un cran au-dessus en ce moment. Une hiérarchie assumée. Toutefois, le joueur a reconnu avec franchise ne pas regarder les matches du club parisien ni son duel face au FC Barcelone, par manque de temps et pour s'occuper de ses enfants.

Une vie de famille bien remplie qui l'éloigne des matches à la télévision, mais qui ne l'empêche pas de maintenir une analyse tranchée sur le rapport de force actuel. Ainsi, malgré les louanges adressées au club de la capitale française, le joueur maintient fermement sa position en faveur du Real Madrid.

Régulièrement aligné avec l'équipe d'Espagne, le latéral gauche prépare un rendez-vous important en Ligue des nations. La Roja disputera en effet la cinquième journée de la compétition le jeudi 12 novembre 2026 sur le terrain de la République tchèque.

Après cette parenthèse internationale, le Real Madrid retrouvera le championnat. Le prochain rendez-vous en club du Real Madrid aura lieu le samedi 21 novembre 2026 au stade Santiago-Bernabéu face au Celta Vigo, à l'occasion d'une nouvelle journée de Liga.

CSS Optimization: Is Using Declarations Just Once (UDJO) Still Effective Today?

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

CSS optimization is a deceptive topic. On the one hand, it feels like something simple that we should already have all the tooling for — especially when it comes to minification. On the other hand, the idea that we already exploited “everything” there is around CSS optimization may be considered naive. Such a claim looks hard to defend for a language that evolves almost daily.

In this article, I’d like to pick a technique that’s at least 18 years old — UDJO, or “using declarations just once.”

Aware of an older source? Please comment!

The technique is inspired by the principle of DRY, “don’t repeat yourself”, in that it works by identifying and reducing (if not eliminating) declaration repetition.

So, if there’s something like display: flex and it appears two or more times, it gets reconciled in one rule:

The outcome of the technique depends on how intelligently the deduplication is done and how long the selectors are. Intelligence determines if UDJO leads to an actual maintainability advantage — can the change of a declaration really be done to all the selectors attached? And the selector length influences how big file size savings are.

If you’re interested, my article on “DRY CSS” describes the mechanics in more detail. The question here is: Is UDJO still effective and still relevant today?

When we try to answer this question, however, we end up with a problem: When sites are complex and their style sheet declarations have not been de-duplicated before, UDJO can require a lot of work.

To save some time, I built tooling for this work. While it didn’t seem like it would immediately be as comprehensive as manually going through and testing style sheets, it seemed realistic enough that a tool could catch some repetition.

Skipping ahead, I created CSS Dedup not only to identify repetition, but also to deduplicate what could safely be optimized (CSS Dedup has some documentation on this but in essence, deduplication affects selector locations, which is part of how the cascade works). This, then, gives us some data:

For the first batch, I arbitrarily picked 20 websites to analyze. I then used CSS Dedup to check what the file size changes would be if we deduplicated what seemed provably safe, and also what seemed probably safe, but only if it led to file size savings.

I picked those two options to provide the “natural” result of deduplication, but also the one site owners may be most interested in to reduce repetition but also decrease transfer size. After all, UDJO as a CSS optimization technique can improve both maintainability and performance.

Important note: These values cannot be used to tell CSS quality—they depend on selector length and also on verifying if deduplication, in the respective case, actually leads to better maintainability. Also note that this leaves out changes after compression—this was not part of the experiment but would be fair to test, too.

For the question whether UDJO still works today, this should be a yes. It works as well as to even come with file size savings.

Maybe you’re curious, too, about what the situation looks like in our field, among web developers. Let’s take some samples on this, too.

It would be valuable to expand this analysis to take more samples and investigate the differences across samples, but there may be something to web professionals using shorter selectors so as to be able to benefit more from UDJO.

As mentioned at the beginning, UDJO’s effectiveness generally depends on the kind of repetition as well as on the length of selectors.

This can be managed, however: While tools like CSS Dedup make no difference between declaration use cases (and are therefore blunt), it is possible to configure them so as to reap the performance benefits.

As the data taken for this article suggest, file size savings may be achieved in most cases. Considering that this is based on safe changes, this means a low-cost performance optimization vector — especially when applied to minified output. It’s still a good idea to couch that against the use of further compression (e.g. gzip/Brotli), but at face value, deduplication shows how it can reduce the size of CSS.

For anyone generally interested in CSS optimization, maintainability, and performance, UDJO should probably be part of the overall approach to CSS. But since the technique has been niche and since tooling is new, I invite you to review, test, and explore this vector. It seems hard to argue that we have reached the ceiling of how far we can take CSS optimization.

Scaloni calls up Molina, Roma deny his departure

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Nahuel Molina has earned a call-up to Scaloni’s Argentina squad after impressing with Gasperini’s Roma in Serie A.

The right-back’s experience with the Albiceleste didn’t end happily after the World Cup.

It’s worth remembering that he initially received seven and then four match suspensions following an appeal by the Argentine Federation.

Scaloni had hoped to have Molina fit for Argentina vs. Costa Rica, once they faced Uruguay, but according to TuttoMercatoWeb.com, the call-up came too late, and Roma will not let him leave.

The Argentina call-up arrives after the deadline, and Roma has informed Nahuel Molina of their decision. The Giallorossi right-back will miss the match against Costa Rica, having been suspended for the Argentina-Uruguay match scheduled for midnight (Italian time) on Saturday, November 14th at “La Bombonera” in Buenos Aires.

The player fully respects the club’s decision not to include him in the November 17th friendly against Costa Rica and will be able to fully prepare for Roma’s upcoming league match away to Parma on Saturday, November 21st at 6:00 PM.

Honda shows off its next electric motorcycle, and it’s wilder than you thought

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Honda has been getting increasingly serious about electric motorcycles, but if you want to see just how weird the company thinks the future could get, take a look at the Honda EV Outlier Concept.

I got a chance to see the wild-looking machine in person at EICMA in Milan, where it looked like almost nothing else on the show floor. And that’s exactly the point.

The EV Outlier Concept originally debuted at last year’s Japan Mobility Show as Honda’s attempt to imagine an electric motorcycle that isn’t simply a conventional motorcycle with the engine swapped for a battery and motor. In fact, Honda describes the concept as looking toward the future of electric motorcycles beyond 2030.

Instead of a centrally mounted motor driving the rear wheel, the EV Outlier uses motors inside both wheels, giving it all-wheel drive and allowing Honda’s designers to completely rethink the rest of the motorcycle’s layout. The battery is housed in that large central black body, while the rider sits extremely low and behind it.

Honda describes the design philosophy with three words: “Gliding, Ecstasy, and Low.” The first refers to the smooth, nearly silent feeling of electric propulsion, while the second describes the instant acceleration and massive torque available from independently controlled front and rear motors. “Low” is more literal: Honda deliberately dropped the rider’s seating and eye position to create an entirely different sensation from a conventional motorcycle.

There’s even a bucket-style backrest designed to support the rider under hard acceleration and let the rider pivot through corners around their hips.

Up front, traditional mirrors are replaced by cameras, while a wide digital cockpit can display things like lean angle, riding modes, front motor torque adjustment, and what the two wheels are doing in real time.

Will Honda actually build this thing? Almost certainly not exactly as you see it here. That’s what concepts are for.

But the EV Outlier is still interesting as a statement of intent. Honda is obviously still playing around with the freedom that electric drivetrains offer, and the company is asking what motorcycles could become once designers no longer have to work around engines, fuel tanks, transmissions, and traditional drivetrains.

And judging by what I saw at EICMA, Honda’s answer could get pretty wild.

Sure, it's still early on in the holiday season, but already we're looking forward to cuddling up with a cup of hot chocolate and an equally sweet Hallmark Christmas movie. Every year, the Hallmark Channel gifts viewers a spirited lineup of holiday movies and romantic comedies to help you get into the yuletide spirit, and 2026 is no exception.

This holiday season, the romance-loving network will debut 20+ original movies in the weeks leading up to December 25, as well as an array of new series and returning TV favorites, in an annual TV extravaganza Hallmark calls the "Countdown to Christmas."

New Hallmark holiday movies are airing every weekend from now through December 21. And don't worry, if you miss any of those titles during their first broadcast, you can catch reruns on Hallmark or stream the flicks on Hallmark Plus the day after their premieres.

Ready to get into the holiday spirit? Mark your calendars because here's the complete 2026 Hallmark Christmas movies guide and schedule.

All movie premieres air at 8 p.m. ET/PT unless otherwise designated.

"Merry Memories"Stars: Lindura, Brendan MorganLogline: Michaela (Lindura) is a rigid accountant who steps in to help her brother care for his young son Danny (Omi Fitzpatrick-Gonzales) while Danny’s military mom is deployed over Christmas. When Danny is assigned to create a holiday scrapbook for his mom, Michaela reluctantly accepts help from his free-spirited substitute teacher, Bryan (Morgan), whose creativity clashes with her need for preciseness and control. As they set out to capture festive moments for the scrapbook unexpected mishaps turn into meaningful traditions – and sparks begin to fly. Through Danny’s project, Michaela learns to embrace imperfection and open her heart, while Bryan rethinks his future as a traveling substitute teacher.

"Holiday Unplugged"Stars: Nikki DeLoach, Juan Pablo Di PaceLogline: Sally (DeLoach), an art teacher, has spent the last 10 years honoring the memory of her late husband Jake while raising their children, Keegan (Brandon Ironside) and Boo (Remy Marthaller). Feeling like she’s finally ready for love, she joins a dating app and is matched with Damon (Di Pace), her former colleague who taught her kids’ kindergarten class. Jake had co-founded a web platform that preserved family home videos. When a server migration wipes out Sally’s treasured videos of him – and her phone containing backup copies accidentally shatters – the only hope is finding the original tapes. She doesn’t want Keegan and Boo to know about the missing videos so to buy time until they’re recovered, Sally institutes a screen-free Christmas for her family while secretly searching for the tapes. After she connects with Damon at his uncle’s antique store, he joins the hunt and the two grow closer. Keegan and Boo soon discover the joys of unplugging and spending quality time with their grandparents, who also come around and embrace the experiment after begrudgingly agreeing. With every lead exhausted, Sally is forced to admit the videos may be gone forever. As the fate of the tapes is in flux, the family learns the importance of cherishing old memories and creating new ones.

"My Christmas Cowboy"Stars: Amy Acker, Luke MacfarlaneLogline: Meg (Acker) set aside her newscaster aspirations after a disastrous audition experience in college and has been happy as a news producer in her small town of Fern, Texas. Billy Kane (Macfarlane), her former college crush and audition partner, is a well-known celebrity host in Hollywood on the brink of losing his career after an on-camera gaffe. At the advice of his publicist, Billy begrudgingly returns to Texas to lay low and spend the holidays with his sister (Canning) and her family. When Meg and Billy cross paths he’s happy to see her but her feelings are on the frostier side – she’s still upset he took advantage of her failed audition to get the gig himself. Meg finds herself seeing Billy more than she’d like since his nephew Graham (Isaiah Haegert) is in the horse therapy program her dad runs at their ranch. With their annual Reindeer Roundup holiday event coming up Meg’s dad gets sidelined after an injury. Her dad enlists Billy to serve as its host, much to Meg’s chagrin. With the Reindeer Roundup just days away, Meg teaches Billy basic riding and roping skills to get him ready for his hosting duties. In the process, they slowly reconnect and Meg sees there’s more to him than Hollywood glitz. As their connection grows, each of them helps give the other the courage to step outside their comfort zones and find new joy – and possibly love – in unexpected ways.

"The Nights Before Christmas"Stars: Tyler Hynes, Laci J MaileyLogline: One Christmas Eve Marty (Hynes), whose life is in a bit of a rut, finds himself wishing he had more time. Thanks to some holiday magic, he awakes the next morning to discover it’s Christmas Eve – again. Confused at first, Marty learns to embrace his new normal and begins to truly appreciate the sights, sounds and people of New York City that previously went unnoticed. With this gift of time, he expands his world by learning how to play a new instrument, picking up new languages and reading…every single book. But when Marty meets Chloe (Mailey), a newly out-of-work journalist having a tough day, he falls hard for her. The only problem is that when the morning of December 24 comes once again, she doesn’t know who he is. As the Christmas Eves continue to repeat themselves, Marty makes it his mission to win her over and, in the process, figure out what he needs to do to finally make his way to Christmas Day – hopefully with Chloe by his side.

"Mistletoe and Mimosas"Stars: Tamera Mowry-Housley, Kristoffer Polaha, Jennifer RobertsonLogline: Claire Bennett (Mowry-Housley) is a successful business consultant in Manhattan who unexpectedly inherits her beloved Uncle Lou’s Christmas-themed bar, The Tipsy Reindeer, in upstate New York. When she returns, Claire discovers the bar comes with high debt – her Uncle Lou was overly generous to people in town while the booming business of Algorithm, an upscale and trendy new club, added insult to injury. She intends to turn things around before Christmas and sell to someone who will preserve her uncle’s legacy and then return to her life in the city. Claire meets Zach (Polaha), Algorithm’s part owner and manager, who tells her they want to buy The Tipsy Reindeer so they can expand. Claire bristles at the proposition and they agree to face off in a 12 Days of Christmas-inspired series of festive events to see who can bring in the most business – if Claire wins, Zach will use his contacts to find the right buyer but if Zach emerges victorious, Claire agrees to consider Algorithm’s offer. As they compete over the course of the 12 days their attraction is undeniable. Rivalry leads to romance and the two are faced with figuring out how their shifting priorities might somehow lead them down the same road, together.

"Our Holiday Playbook"Stars: Taylor Cole, Charles Michael Davis.Logline: When Lexi Bell (Cole) returns home for the holidays, she faces a beloved family tradition she’d rather avoid: the fiercely competitive Bell Bowl, an annual Christmas flag football showdown between her father and uncle that has fueled decades of sibling rivalry. But when an injury sidelines her brother-in-law it looks like Lexi’s family’s losing streak will remain unbroken. To give them a fighting chance, she strikes a deal with Eddie (Davis), a former local football star-turned teacher – Lexi will use her baking know-how to help Eddie best his brother in their annual bake-off if he’ll pose as her boyfriend and meet the “eligibility requirement” to play on their team. As they navigate each other’s festive family gatherings, traditions and celebrations, what started as a transactional partnership begins to become something more as Lexi and Eddie find themselves feeling less and less like their romantic relationship is just pretend. As the two clans battle it out on the field, regardless of who takes home the trophy Lexi may have already won at love.

"Christmas in Blue Dog Valley"Stars: Erin Cahill, Andrew WalkerLogline: On-set veterinarian Goldie MacKenzie (Cahill) leaves the glamour of Hollywood behind to buy a family-run veterinary clinic in tight-knit Blue Dog Valley, arriving just in time for the town’s cherished Christmas season. When Dr. Gable, the clinic’s longtime owner, unexpectedly ends up out of town, Goldie is hosted by his handsome son Cohen (Walker), the black sheep of the family because he decided to forego their expectation of becoming a vet. Cohen and Goldie get off on the wrong foot, but when the folks of Blue Dog Valley cold shoulder “the newcomer,” Cohen teams up with Goldie to win over even the frostiest of community members. Goldie is also aided by the mysterious canine she dubs Blue whose spontaneous appearances always seem to guide Goldie to exactly where she needs to be. As Goldie works hard to prove herself to the townspeople when furry and Christmas crises arise, she finds herself pulled towards the very heart of her new-found home...and a certain black sheep.

"Double Booked for the Holidays"Stars: Rachael Leigh Cook, Owain Yeoman, Stephen HaganLogline: There’s only one thing identical twins, quiet and bookish Abigail (Cook) and globe-trotting event planner Charlie (Cook), agree on is that this first Christmas without their mom will be difficult. When they unexpectedly get a last-minute chance to travel to Wales and spend a week running the Story Shoppe, an AirBnb/bookshop in Hay-on-Wye, they jump at the opportunity. Abigail is particularly excited, given the town’s role as the backdrop of the bestselling Winterton Chronicles, her all-time favorite Regency holiday romances. Upon arrival, Abigail dives into setting up the bookshop’s window display with help from the friendly next-door barista, Tom (Yeoman), who is also a fan of the Winterton Chronicles. Meanwhile, Charlie meets the dashing Lord Rhys (Hagan), the great-nephew of Evelyn Calon, who wrote the book series. Abigail is charmed by a series of thoughtful Christmas ornaments she assumes are from Rhys (but are actually from Tom), while Charlie and Rhys grow closer as they help plan the Winterton Ball at Rhys’ castle. Mistaken identities lead to a series of merry mix-ups as Rhys and Tom woo the sisters, unaware they are twins. On the night of the ball, however, the sisters’ double secret is discovered. As the truth is unwrapped, the crisscrossed lovers discover that the perfect holiday happy ending isn’t always by the book.

"Christmas Delivered"Stars: Autumn Reeser, Wes BrownLogline: Determined to unwind from the stresses and loneliness of her everyday life, Kimberly Carr (Reeser) travels to Canada to spend Christmas with her grandmother, JoJo (Linda Darlow), at her beautiful, cozy farmhouse. Kimberly's plans for a restorative holiday are turned upside down when she crosses paths with Ben Chambers (Brown), owner of a local tree farm and new Christmas tree delivery app called TreeBen. Ben's a dreamer who never looks before he leaps while Kimberly is methodical and lives her life based on practicality – the two couldn't be more different. But when Ben’s app is plagued by an onslaught of issues, Kimberly uses her tech expertise to help him out and ensure that no one in town is deprived of a Christmas tree...even though she isn’t convinced of the need for the app in such a small town. As they team up to deliver trees in his red truck, Kimberly and Ben discover they may have more in common than they realized. In the process, Kimberly begins to question if she’s truly happy with her life or if she’s ready for a change and – just maybe – ready for romance.

"The Christmas Eve Feast"*Premieres 6pm ET/PTStars: Daniella Monet, Kevin McGarryLogline: Silvana “Silvy” Gianetti (Monet) is disillusioned with her job as the Executive Chef at a swanky restaurant in New York. Growing up in a large and loving Italian-American family, Silvy is drawn to the food and comfort of her home. When her beloved Nonna suffers a minor wrist injury, Silvy is called back home to Boston’s Italian North End to prepare the family’s beloved “Feast of the Seven Fishes” Christmas Eve dinner. As she preps for the big feast, her family is joined by Ethan (McGarry), a photographer who is documenting different family traditions around town and who’s become close to her Nonna. After a rocky start with Ethan, things begin to heat up as Silvy grows to appreciate the heart and soul he puts into capturing the magic of the little moments and a romance could be simmering. Every day leading up to Christmas Eve, Silvy gathers in the kitchen with her Nonna, mom and aunt to prepare the pasta, struffoli and other Italian classics near and dear to Silvy’s heart. This special family time reminds her of the kind of cooking that truly brings her joy and leads Silvy to rethink where she belongs and gets a clear picture of who should be by her side.

"Return to Santa"Stars: Paul Campbell, Rachel BostonLogline: Hopeful and dreamy eight-year-old Jake (Campbell) writes a letter to Santa with all his most fantastical wishes. But when the letter gets lost in the North Pole, young Jake wakes up disappointed, and no longer a believer. Decades later Santa discovers the misplaced letter and immediately sets out to make things right. Now an adult, Jake’s life is about to get turned upside down when the appearance of his childhood wishes clash with his practical life. All while his childhood best friend Julie (Boston) rather unexpectedly moved back to town. As Santa continues to check off Jake’s list, Jake and Julie combine forces to anticipate each new wishes’ chaotic consequences, all while growing closer to each other – and possibly leading Jake closer to his most important childhood dream.

"The Most Wonderful Secret"*Premieres 6pm ET/PTStars: Tammin Sursok, Peter PorteLogline: Every Christmas, a small town honors the memory of a beloved teacher who once spread joy through her “Secret Norma” gifts each holiday by creating a whimsical and heartfelt scavenger hunt for one worthy recipient. When burned-out art teacher Vanessa (Sursok) visits her widowed brother and niece Esther for the holidays, she is surprised to be chosen as the next “Secret Norma” recipient. Hoping to bring Esther some Christmas magic, Vanessa decides to help revive the town’s holiday block party. Her handsome electrician neighbor Spencer (Porte) offers help with the preparations if Vanessa will help him sell his mother Norma’s house. As Vanessa solves the riddles of “Secret Norma” and plans the block party, her passion for teaching art begins to spark along with her feelings for the charming electrician next door.

"Holiday Ever After: A Disney World Wish Come True"Stars: Lacey Chabert, Travis Van Winkle, Richard Kind, Christy Carlson Romano, Bryce Durfee, Taegen Burns, Asher AlexanderLogline: Lindsey (Lacey Chabert) and her extended family head to Walt Disney World to spend a magical Christmas together. But the dream of a jolly holiday gets quickly dashed when Lindsey discovers her room is next door to Philip (Travis Van Winkle), a disastrous first date she recently had, who’s also there on a family trip. As Lindsey and Philip’s paths cross throughout their stay, the frost starts to thaw as they learn they’re not so different from each other after all. Thanks to some Walt Disney World magic, the holiday wish Lindsey made in Cinderella Fountain just might turn their rivalry into romance.

"Eight Nights for Love"*Premieres 6pm ET/PTStars: Hunter King, Dan JeannotteLogline: Hannah (King), who works for a large auction house in New York City, is excited for her best friend Ethan (Jeannotte) to return home for a visit during Hannukah and is looking forward to some much-needed time off from work That plan is short lived when Ethan shares the news that he’s convinced the daughter of his wealthy boss to use Hannah’s auction house to sell their family’s collection of Judaica with the condition that Hannah handles the sale. Complicating her holiday further, she meets the mysterious Pearl (Felicia Shulman), who gifts her with an antique menorah that is anything but ordinary as it takes her back to a different times of her life each night of Hanukkah, with Pearl as her guide. This front row seat past moments – including some that involve Ethan – leads Hannah to reflect, break out of her comfort zone and overcome her fear of failure. Thanks to her trips through the past, and as she and Ethan spend time together in the present Hannah realizes she has feelings for him she never acknowledged. Through this journey, Hannah makes some bold decisions about her life – and love – that are sure to make this Hanukkah one to remember.

"Miles to Christmas"Stars: Ashley Williams, Steve Lund, Alison SweeneyLogline: When an early snowstorm sweeps through the Midwest, punctual, straight-laced “suit” Cassidy Fields (Williams) is forced to hitch a ride with free spirited, music loving Johanna “Jo” Woods (Sweeney) in her bother Chase’s (Lund) 18-wheeler to the nearest airport to catch another flight. However, when Jo’s flight successfully takes off and Cassidy’s is cancelled again at the last minute, she joins Chase on the road back to Tennessee from Chicago to make it home in time to spend Christmas with her father. Quickly, Cassidy and Chase’s connection begins to spark. Through losing the car keys, stopping to partake in a Mrs. Claus parade and a surprise run-in with Jo, whose plane got grounded mid-flight due to weather, Cassidy builds bonds with both siblings as they all challenge one another’s life philosophies. As the trio pushes past every obstacle, they work to embrace their differences and find that the fastest way home is when they lean on one another for help.

"The Snowflake Effect"Stars: Heather Hemmens, Mark GhaniméLogline: When driven corporate attorney Tanya (Hemmens) rushes into a charming gift shop searching for a last-minute White Elephant gift, she crosses paths with Jason (Ghanimé), a handsome, laid-back clerk. Their brief encounter leads Tanya to purchase a beautiful antique glass snowflake which, unbeknownst to her or Jason, is a good luck charm that carries a generations-old Christmas tradition. However, since the ornament is entered into a holiday gift exchange rather than gifted with proper intention, Tanya suddenly finds herself on the receiving end of everyone else’s misfortune. After Jason’s Aunt Coco (Jayne Eastwood) explains the snowflake’s magical lore, Tanya reluctantly teams up with Jason to track down the snowflake as it makes its way from one recipient to the next across Chicago before Christmas Day or she’ll be stuck with bad luck until next Christmas. Along the way, the pair is swept into festive holiday adventures, unexpected encounters and a journey that challenges Tanya’s carefully structured life. As they race against the clock to undo the snowflake’s magic, Tanya and Jason’s relationship turns into something more and they may find that their biggest stroke of luck was meeting each other.

"A Grand Biltmore Christmas" Stars: Holland Roden, Niall Matter, Andie MacDowell, James Trevena, Jonathan Frakes, Rachel Boston, Wes Brown.Logline: As Biltmore House opens for the first time on Christmas Eve 1895, 13-year-old Elizabeth and her family are holiday guests. When she notices Michael, a fellow teen caught trespassing by the staff, Elizabeth comes to his rescue by claiming to know him, much to his relief. The two share an instant connection and set out to explore Biltmore but Michael inexplicably vanishes. Twenty years later, Elizabeth (Roden) is spending Christmas at Biltmore and this time is accompanied by James (Trevena), a suitor her Aunt Maysilee (MacDowell), is keen for her to wed. When a stranger asks Elizabeth to dance at the Christmas gala she’s shocked to discover it’s Michael (Matter). Thrilled to reunite with her friend, she throws herself into his arms. But the world around her changes in an instant when Elizabeth is transported into the modern world with Michael, a tourist from the present day who had traveled to the past 20 years prior with the help of a magical hourglass in Biltmore’s library. Now, Elizabeth and Michael must find a way to send her back to her time, while not arousing the suspicion of Michael’s mother and sister during their family vacation. As Elizabeth experiences life and Christmas in the 21st century, she and Michael become close. When the time comes for her to return to 1915, Elizabeth must decide whether she’ll resume the life that’s been chosen for her or if her heart will lead her down a new path in the present.SUNDAY, DEC. 6

"An Angel in My Stocking"Stars: Fionna Gubelmann, Marco Grazzini, Siobhan Williams, Nancy Robertson Logline: Amanda (Gubelmann) and Jack (Grazzini) had a whirlwind romance and got married after knowing each other for only three months. Their first – and only – Christmas together was calamitous and was the beginning of the end to their brief marriage. Three years post-divorce and still harboring an aversion to the holiday, Amanda is heading to Vermont with her sister Lucy (Williams) to enjoy an un-Christmasy getaway…and finds that Jack is on their flight. En route, Amanda meets Margaret (Kane), a quirky traveler who disappears without a trace and without being seen by anyone else, leaving Amanda mystified. Bad weather diverts the plane to the remote town of Pinecone Lake, leaving Amanda with the last person she wants to be stranded with – Jack. Shortly after arrival, Margaret inexplicably reappears and convinces a confused Amanda that she is in fact her guardian angel and has been her entire life. As the days leading to Christmas pass by, Amanda slowly begins to feel connected to the community and, with Margaret’s guidance, sees beyond past circumstances and follows her heart to where – and with whom – she truly belongs.

"Christmas in Canterbury"Stars: Jessy Schram, Will KempLogline: Rose (Schram) arrives in Canterbury, England eager to spend the holidays with her childhood friend Asha (Sohm Kapila) as she leaves behind her hometown of Vienna, OH following the passing of her beloved Grandma Bud. Having first met in a children’s choir they went on to pursue careers as choir masters. As Rose arrives Asha is put on bed rest in the final days of her pregnancy. Rose steps in when the headmistress offers her the job…not realizing there was confusion over which Vienna children’s choir she worked with. Rose’s “glass half full” attitude is tested when she crosses paths with Harrison (Kemp), the traditionalist boys’ choir master who has an unwavering allegiance to history and precedents. The boys’ choir always performs at the annual Christmas Eve concert in Canterbury Cathedral but Rose questions why the girls don’t have the opportunity. When the school’s headmistress decides the two choirs should compete against each other for the honor, they get to work to prepare their students. Along the way, their rivalry sparks a romance and these two seemingly opposites from opposite sides of the pond discover they may not be so different after all.

"Snow Globe Town"Stars: Kimberley Sustad, Scott Michael FosterLogline: Inspired by Brad Paisley’s song “Snow Globe Town,” this heartfelt holiday romance follows Emily Church (Sustad), who joins her sister Zoe and Zoe’s longtime boyfriend on a Christmas getaway to Pine Grove, the town famously known as the Christmas Proposal Capital of America. Expecting to help capture a photos of Zoe’s perfect engagement moment as is their family’s tradition, Emily instead finds herself unexpectedly sidelined by the town’s couples-only traditions and paired with Bert Baxter (Foster), a skeptical journalist researching the celebrated romantic legacy inspired by his grandfather. "Noelle Nomads"Stars: Benjamin Ayres, Torrey DeVitto, Holland Roden.Logline: Joseph has to deliver a gift from his late father to a campground, but things go wrong at every turn. With the help of Nora, he learns to embrace nature and its beauty.

"Barking All the Way"Stars: Ian Harding, Tori AndersonLogline: Billie (Anderson) is a workaholic realtor who heads home for Christmas but her journey is derailed after loveable, but chaotic canine named Yappy almost causes her car to crash. Survive as the two lone, single people and avoid family matchmaking. Susie finds herself turning over a new leaf in pursuing romance while Alec discovers love can walk into your life when you least expect it.

The Buffett Indicator Is Flashing Red. These 3 Dividend Stocks Could Be Safe Havens.

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The stock market is looking overvalued, according to this indicator.

Investors listen to Warren Buffett because he has demonstrated his sharp understanding of the stock market over time. Leading Berkshire Hathaway for six decades, he delivered market-beating returns. Buffett retired at the start of the year, but today he's chairman emeritus and remains involved in the investing world.

And Buffett's words of wisdom still ring true, meaning it's an excellent idea to refer to them as needed when investing. Right now may be a particularly good time to do this. This is because Buffett once said the market was "playing with fire" when it did something that it's doing again right now. The Buffett indicator, a measure of the stock market's total value in relation to the size of the U.S. economy, has surpassed 200%. In fact, it's now at 281%. This signals that stocks might be overvalued relative to economic output.

In such an environment, with the Buffett indicator flashing red, declines may be on the horizon. Here are three dividend stocks, companies that offer you passive income, that could be safe havens.

Target (TGT +4.73%) has proven its commitment to rewarding shareholders as it has lifted its dividend for more than 50 straight years -- and this has landed it on the list of Dividend Kings. With such a track record, it's likely the company will aim to maintain this momentum. So, when you buy Target stock, you can be pretty confident that you'll see dividend growth year after year. This year, Target paid out a little more than $1 billion in dividends in the first half.

The retail giant pays a dividend of $4.64 per share, representing a yield of 2.9%, greatly surpassing the S&P 500 dividend yield of about 1%.

Investors also will like Target's recovery story. The company has struggled to grow in recent years amid various headwinds, but new chief Michael Fiddelke launched a plan earlier this year that's starting to bear fruit. The company is revamping store displays and refreshing merchandise, leaning into AI to improve growth, and lowering prices on many items. This, along with Target's offering of essentials, may boost revenue in the quarters to come.

Johnson & Johnson (JNJ +2.13%) is another Dividend King you can count on for passive income growth and earnings strength. The pharma giant pays a dividend of $5.36 per share, at a yield of 1.9%. The company's free cash flow of more than $19 billion on a trailing 12-month basis shows it has the financial strength to keep this going.

You'll also like J&J for its leadership in pharmaceutical products, through its innovative medicine business, and in medtech. Including the impact of currencies, these businesses helped J&J's revenue to advance 6% in the recent quarter to $25 billion. This, along with 29 products generating $1 billion or more in revenue annually, put the company on track for more than $100 billion in revenue this year -- that's a first for J&J. These current products, as well as a strong pipeline, are reason to be confident that growth may continue from this point. And that makes J&J an excellent stock to add to your portfolio in any environment.

Coca-Cola (KO +0.38%) is another Dividend King -- and this company also is a longtime Warren Buffett favorite. Buffett added the beverage giant to his portfolio in the late 1980s, and it remains one of Berkshire Hathaway's top holdings.

You probably know Coca-Cola best for its eponymous beverage, but it also sells a wide variety of well-known drinks across the non-alcoholic spectrum, including Fuze tea and Dasani water. Its commitment to classics such as Coca-Cola, its moves to offer products designed to suit the needs of individual markets, and marketing efforts to keep the brand fresh in consumers' minds have helped the company grow revenue over the years.

As for the dividend, Coca-Cola pays $2.12 per share, at a yield of 2.2%, and thanks to its financial strength and track record, the company is likely to keep the growth going well into the future. So, with the Buffett indicator flashing red, this stock is a fantastic safe haven.

Adria Cimino has positions in Target. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Berkshire Hathaway and Target. The Motley Fool recommends Johnson & Johnson. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

Don't be fooled by Horses of Hoofprint Bay's hand-drawn art, this stable management game has all the drama of a Jilly Cooper novel

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Poema is my pride and joy. She was my first horse and she's a competition winner, but she's reached her potential. She'll never be a show jumper, and that's where the money and prestige are to be found. If I'm ever to grow my horse farm into a nationally renowned equestrian centre, I need the cash to expand and upgrade my facilities. Poema is a hoofstep on the path to that future, but she's not going to be able to take me all the way herself.

For all its hand-drawn aesthetics, Horses of Hoofprint Bay has hard realities like the limits of Poema's future bubbling under its surface. While the game's scripted narrative may be lacking, it's shockingly easy to fill in the gaps with drama worthy of a Jilly Cooper novel.

There may be better author comparisons, but I learned everything I know about showjumping from Cooper's Riders – much as I learned everything about independent television franchise bids in the '80s from its sequel Rivals – a book which is as much about the nitty gritty of horsetraining and competing as it is about toffs jumping into bed with one another. In my mind the two are tightly connected - showjumping and Jilly Cooper, not horses and toff bonking. In Horses of Hoofprint Bay, the only real story you get is the fact you've scrimped and saved to buy your first horse and your uncle has lent you the use of his field while you start your business. I was hoping there would at least be a similar setup to Regency Solitaire, where your motivation for touring the country's tearooms is motivated by paying off your brother's gambling debts. Yet, despite this disappointing start, I soon found its business simulation mechanics provide great storytelling material.

Horses of Hoofprint Bay has many of the trappings of a cosy game. At the start of each day, you'll prepare the stables, dragging a rake around the yard to sling away the manure, clicking buckets of water to refill the trough, and making sure there's enough hay for all the horses. But this isn't just for the larks, if you skip these tasks there are serious knock on effects for yours horses. Forget to brush down your horses' coats or pick out the stones in their shoes, and they're liable to develop saddle sores and other injuries when you ride them. Leave an abrasion untreated and it can develop into an infection. Even something like taking a dirty horse to a contest can cost you. I forgot to brush Poema before entering her into her first contest and despite taking first place, her embarrassingly unkempt coat lost my stable prestige. It was a cruel end to what should have been a great celebration.

So long as your horses are well rested, you can train them each day. You may want to take them through some confidence building lunges, where they need to walk, trot, and canter on demand, or a more physically demanding Cavaletti or showjumping course. These latter too are much more tiring, but they build a horse's strength and endurance. The higher its endurance the more energy it can expend on training sessions each day, and the higher its strength the more jumps it can do in succession before slowing to a walk for a breather - essential when you're competing in a timed competition. Overdo the exercise, however, and you'll exhaust a horse, severely reducing its energy for the next day.

Now, unlike Poema, my freshly bought stallion Kolizey has the potential to compete in showjumping competitions. He'll never go all the way to the big national contests, but he can take a ribbon in the district and regional contests. Or he will when he's up to standard; he's arrived from the breeder in a sorry state: brambles in his mane, mud on his flanks, stones in his shoes, and his strength and endurance are far too low to make it through an entire course of obstacles.

The biggest problem, however, is Kolizey's confidence: it's shot. I don't know what was happening in the stalls at the breeder's, but he arrived at my stables backing up at the first sign of a jump. Before I can hope to earn him his first ribbon, I have to build up our relationship. If we exercise together enough in the yard, his confidence will grow and he will follow my direction without question.

It may be getting ahead of myself, but I also have plans for Kolizey and Poema to produce a foal. Done right, the two should raise a horse that's able to exceed its parents' potential, becoming stronger and more endurant than both of them. A stronger horse means a ticket to the bigger contests and the bigger prize pots. Not that I can start breeding the two just yet. I want both horses to be at peak physical fitness. Poema's already there, while Kolizey can't even manage a jump without shying away.

While getting Koliszey contest ready, I still need to be bringing in cash. That means using Poema as a training horse for students' riding lessons. The better I am at the minigame where I use the Q, E, W, A, S, and D keys to tell them to sit forward or back, hold the reins higher or lower, or to level out their legs, the more they tip me. I have to be careful, because when Poema is working with students, she's not being stretched, and her strength and endurance can wither which means she struggles to maintain her winning streak in the contests.

Another reason to hold off on getting Poema pregnant is that during the second half of her pregnancy, she can't be ridden, which means no contests and no lessons for students. Currently, those are my two main sources of income besides what I'm paid in rent to board a couple of nearby farmers' horses. I don't want her out of the rota when Kolizey isn't yet ready to step into her place.

And, well, while I'm on the subject of plans, reader, I must tell you, the future lies in semen. If I can build the facilities, earn the cash, and raise Kolizey's profile, I can make a good buck on his virile stallion cream. With the money I bring in from a prizewinning semen I can expand into the far field, creating space to take on more horses, upgrade my home from the caravan I'm living in currently, maybe even build a proper training school. I'm not going to afford all that by giving riding lessons to the local children or boarders' rent. No, the big money comes from competition wins and valuable horse sauce.

While Poema continues entering contests and carrying students, I start pushing Kolizey harder. Multiple training sessions every day. I begin with Cavaletti, which is a kind of obedience competition. Your horse has to be travelling at the correct speed when it passes marker flags – walking, trotting, or cantering – and make some small jumps over cross bars. It should be straightforward, but because Kolizey's confidence is so low, he keeps refusing my orders and trying to change speed. He warns me before he's about to speed up or slow down, and if I counter it in time he will obey me and even receive a little confidence boost, but often he does it just before a jump, messing up my timing and he hits the bars. Each time he fails a jump it knocks his confidence, risking me taking me even further from where I need him to be.

I try the easier lunges sessions. And they raise his confidence a little but they're doing little for his physical health. It's all taking too long and Poema's getting older every day. In a fit of frustration, and in mark that I may not be able to cut it as an animal trainer, I skip from the Cavaletti training straight into a showjumping qualifier. The time limits on these are much stricter, so you have to clear the course at a gallop. This is also where a horse's strength really comes into play. The faster you're going, the more strength it requires to jump an obstacle. Horses only recover strength when moving at a walk, so you don't want to tire a horse out by going at a gallop through the whole course, else they won't have the strength to make all the jumps and they'll be forced to withdraw from the event. But nor do you want to go too slowly and fall foul of the time limit.

I realise my mistake as soon as the course begins: I try to stir Kolizey into a walk he refuses. Thankfully the timer only starts when he crosses the line, but it's an inauspicious start. I tap forward again and he begins to walk. I tap again and Kolizey moves up to a trot, then to a canter, before picking up to a gallop. I think he's going too fast as we approach the first jump, but Kolizey clears it. Then another jump and another. With a nervous eye on the depleting strength bar, I tap him down to a canter for the next jump. We're halfway through the course, I briefly slow to a walk to recover some strength then take the next three jumps at a canter. We're so close to the end now but a big yellow exclamation mark appears next to the strength bar. Kolizey is on the edge of exhaustion but he refuses to slow down and increases to a gallop for the last two jumps.

He completes the course with a perfect clear. Not a single obstacle clipped and we end with ten seconds left on the timer. Not only has Kolizey earned his showjumping license, his success swells his confidence. He's a natural athlete.

The next day, with my horses rested, I take Kolizey and Poema to town to compete in the showjumping and Caprilli events respectively. They both bring home first place, raising the prestige of my stables, earning me a tidy little packet, and convincing me it's time my prized horses started a family. I'm perhaps too eager, when I try to send them to the breeding station, I'm told they're both 'Done for the day'. I suppose I should give my contest winners a bit of a break. They've earned it. I groom them and send them both to bed with an apple as a treat.

Except, maybe they weren't too tired: the next morning, I'm met with a message telling me Poema is pregnant. My stable's become the Olympic Village, with my rosette-winning horses working out the highs and stresses of competition in the only way they know how. Good on them. I needn't have even made the breeding station.

Then, with horror, I remember the horses I'm boarding for the neighbours. They're both stallions themselves. Neither of them are at competition level, their strength and endurance is lower even than Poema's when she arrived at the stable. If either of them are the father, Poema is about to be out of action for a whole season and at the other end of it I'll have a foal that can't even compete in the beginner events. fetch the equestrian version of Hercule Poirot, I need to find out who impregnated my horse!

It may not be a plot worthy of Cooper, but it will do for me.

Rock Paper Shotgun is better when you sign in

Sign in and join us on our journey to discover strange and compelling PC games.

United Pilots Squawk Hijack Code As Passenger Tries To Storm Cockpit, Says He Has A Bomb

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

A United Airlines flight from Newark Liberty International Airport (EWR) to Louis Armstrong New Orleans International Airport (MSY) took an eventful turn on Monday evening after a passenger reportedly tried to storm the cockpit. The alarming occurrence led the pilots to alert other United flights to the threat via the ACARS messaging system, with the flight ultimately landing safely in New Orleans.

Still, while this particular incident was resolved safely, the response of the crew, which included issuing the squawk code 7500 to indicate unlawful interference, shows how seriously such threats are taken. While important everywhere, this is particularly timely at United, given that it was only last month that one of its planes had to divert after a passenger was seen sending texts about hijacking the aircraft.

United Airlines flight UA2174 is a regularly scheduled evening flight from Newark to New Orleans. On Monday, November 9, data from Flightradar24 shows that it took off two minutes early at 9:26 PM, and touched down 52 minutes ahead of schedule at 11:07 PM. However, during the flight, as flagged by FlightDeck on X, a male passenger is said to have tried to storm the Boeing 737 MAX 9's cockpit.

The flight's pilots squawked 7500 to alert air traffic control to the threat, and later reported that he had been wearing dark clothing and was carrying a backpack that he said contained a bomb. In an ACARS message to other United jets, they said that "we had an attempted level four, no other details are known at this time." 7500 was first squawked as the jet descended through 20,000 feet, and it landed safely.

Simple Flying has reached out to United Airlines for futher information on this incident. We will update our coverage upon receiving a response from the carrier.

By the time that N17422, the Boeing 737 MAX 9 operating United Airlines flight UA2174, had arrived at its gate in New Orleans on Monday night, had arrived at its gate, the passenger in question had been taken to the back of the jet and was sitting with the cabin crew. Few other details are known at this point, but, given the serious nature of his actions on board, police officers were likely waiting for him.

Airlines are always vigilant when it comes to the threat of hijack situations, but, at United Airlines, the danger is particularly timely. After all, it was just last month that one of its aircraft had to be diverted and locked down after a flight attendant saw a passenger sending texts about hijacking the plane. In this instance, the jet was a Boeing 737 MAX 8 (as seen above) that was flying from Las Vegas to Newark.

With the suspicious message having been spotted shortly after takeoff, the jet did not continue to its journey as planned, but, rather, returned to Harry Reid International Airport (LAS), where it was met by law enforcement. According to People Magazine, the texter, a 25-year-old male passenger, was under the influence of alcohol and was later arrested for making false threats regarding terrorism.

A Las Vegas flight turned back after cabin crew spotted a disturbing text and officers met the aircraft on return.

In recent times, 'squawk 7500' has become an increasingly common phrase in coverage of such alarming incidents as these. Arguably the most notable this year was the attempted hijacking of flydubai flight FZ1073 on its way from Dubai International Airport (DXB) to Tel Aviv Ben Gurion Airport (TLV). This flight issued the squawk codes 7700 and 7500, but what do they mean, and why are they so important?

Found an error? Send it info@simpleflying.com so it can be corrected.

Squawk codes are essentially four-digit numbers that pilots can set their aircraft's transponder to in order to indicate their status to ATC. 7700 is used as a general emergency indicator, highlighting that a plane needs priority treatment. 7500, meanwhile, shows a hijacking or unlawful interference, and it is a way of alerting ATC non-verbally without the hijackers knowing that controllers are aware of the situation.

Mauricio Pochettino’s ‘Preferred’ Man Utd Starting XI Predicted

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Mauricio Pochettino has emerged as the leading contender to replace Michael Carrick at Manchester United, and fans are subsequently starting to speculate about how the former Tottenham Hotspur boss might set his team up at Old Trafford.

The Red Devils have endured a dismal start to the new campaign, winning just three of their first 15 matches across all competitions. They head into the November international break in an unprecedented position — in the relegation zone after ten Premier League games.

Carrick has been walking on a tightrope for several weeks now, and Sunday evening's chaotic encounter with Aston Villa has seemingly proved to be the breaking point for the United hierarchy. Reports emerged on Monday evening suggesting that the north-west club plan to relieve their former midfielder of his duties during this two-week interlude in the season.

Pochettino has emerged as a surprise frontrunner for the job, with United said to be weighing up whether to activate the United States Men's National Team's $10 million release clause (via Ben Jacobs).

Should they do so, and should the Argentine accept the proposal on the table, it'll be his first managerial role at club level since he parted ways with Chelsea at the end of the 2023/24 season.

Inheriting a mess with the USMNT, Pochettino slowly rebuilt the feeling around the nation's set-up, before unleashing a highly competent and respectable outfit at the World Cup in the summer.

The scale of the task at United is a lot bigger, but the premise is the same. Rebuild and revitalise a broken and fractured team, culturally and tactically.

Excited United supporters are unsurprisingly focusing on the latter aspect of Pochettino's potential job, amid reports that he is now the leading candidate. Preferred starting elevens are already circulating in Red Devils circles, with fans theorising what their team might look like under the ex-Paris Saint-Germain coach.

United content creator, AliceTalksFooty, published a mock-up of what she believes Pochettino could opt for. Several big names miss out, including the ever-present Harry Maguire and new signing Youri Tielemans.

Senne Lammens is predicted to retain his position in between the sticks, despite his dubious recent form. Like the Belgian, Diogo Dalot's performances in recent weeks have been heavily scrutinised, but the Portuguese is still tipped to start at right-back, with Patrick Dorgu holding down the other flank as the other 'athletic' full-back.

Interestingly, Matthijs de Ligt is favoured over Maguire in this concept eleven. This is despite the Dutchman hardly featuring over the last 12 months — he hasn't played a minute of Premier League football since November 2025 due to a back injury.

Elsewhere, Tielemans' potential absence in the middle of the park will come as a surprise to many. United forked out £35 million for the 29-year-old in the summer, yet the blend of attributes in midfield with himself and Bruno Fernandes doesn't feel right.

Many feel that having both start leaves United vulnerable defensively, particularly in transition, and this has certainly been reflected in the Red Devils' dire defensive record thus far this season.

Instead, Kobbie Mainoo and Carlos Baleba are favoured, providing Fernandes with a platform to express himself further up the pitch.

Antonio Conte is believed to be a top target for Man Utd to replace Michael Carrick, and this is how he would likely line up his side if he took over.

While many believe Marcus Rashford could be a favourite of Pochettino's, Matheus Cunha is still tipped to hold down the left-wing spot. Benjamin Sesko, who is currently struggling with an injury, is backed to be the 2019 Champions League finalists' first-choice striker, ousting Bryan Mbeumo out to the right wing.

It remains to be seen whether this set-up resembles what a Pochettino United team might actually look like, although the core feeling that he might ruffle a few feathers in a divided dressing room with several big egos is almost certainly accurate.

7 AI Automations SMEs Can Launch in a Week (No Full Engineering Team Required)

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 10 • Originally published at nextctl.hashnode.dev

Small businesses don't need complicated AI systems to automate useful work. They need one recurring problem, a clear workflow, and a way to catch mistakes before customers notice them.

When evaluating AI agents for small business, I recommend starting with a measurable problem rather than an ambitious autonomous system.

Here are seven automations SMEs can test in a five-day sprint using existing software, lightweight AI services, and minimal engineering support.

Before building anything, assign one person to own the workflow. Follow three rules:

Tools such as n8n can connect systems through APIs, webhooks, and scheduled workflows. Add AI only where interpretation or summarization helps.

For teams exploring AI automation for SMEs, reliable execution matters more than impressive demonstrations.

Missed calls shouldn't disappear into phone logs nobody checks. An AI receptionist can help capture inquiries and organize follow-ups.

Trigger: An incoming business call goes unanswered, or a voicemail arrives.

Action: Extract available information into structured fields: caller name and number, reason for calling, requested service, and preferred callback time. Create a CRM note or follow-up task. Mark unavailable details as unknown rather than inventing them.

Human checkpoint: A team member reviews uncertain transcripts and returns calls.

Measure: Missed calls converted into actionable follow-up records.

Customers ask questions outside office hours. Answer routine questions automatically while protecting situations requiring judgment.

Trigger: A visitor submits a website chat or messaging inquiry after hours.

Action: Search an approved FAQ covering business hours, services, appointments, and verified company information. Respond only when the answer is supported. Otherwise, collect contact details and create a follow-up request.

Human checkpoint: Escalate complaints, sensitive questions, pricing exceptions, and anything outside the approved knowledge base.

Measure: Questions answered versus questions escalated.

Fake submissions waste sales time and make CRM reporting unreliable.

Trigger: Someone submits a website contact form.

Action: Check for missing required fields, invalid email formats, duplicate submissions, and suspicious links or repetitive messages. Use simple validation rules first; AI can help classify ambiguous submissions. Route entries into accepted, review, or suspected-spam categories.

Human checkpoint: Review uncertain submissions before rejecting them. Legitimate inquiries sometimes contain unusual information.

Measure: Spam reaching the CRM and legitimate leads incorrectly flagged.

Meeting preparation shouldn't require searching through scattered emails and customer records.

Trigger: A scheduled customer meeting approaches.

Action: Retrieve relevant calendar details, recent CRM notes, previous interactions, and outstanding tasks. Generate a brief with the meeting purpose, last customer request, outstanding commitments, and suggested discussion points. Keep factual statements traceable to their sources.

Human checkpoint: The meeting owner reviews the brief. Restrict access to authorized customer records.

Measure: Preparation time and brief accuracy.

Sales opportunities sometimes go quiet because follow-ups get overlooked.

Trigger: A CRM opportunity has no recorded activity beyond a defined period.

Action: Check its stage, last interaction, owner, and pending tasks. Draft a short follow-up using verified information, then create an approval task.

Measure: Approved follow-ups completed and conversations restarted.

Administrative follow-ups are easy to overlook when teams handle multiple invoices and proposals.

Trigger: An invoice or proposal reaches its configured follow-up date.

Action: Read its current status from the accounting or proposal system. If still pending, create an internal notification containing the reference, recipient, due date, and last recorded action. Use the system of record for financial information rather than AI-generated calculations.

Human checkpoint: An operations or finance team member verifies the status before contacting anyone. Don't automate payment disputes, contractual interpretations, or collection decisions. This is operational assistance, not legal advice.

Measure: Pending records reviewed on schedule.

Automations sometimes fail quietly when API credentials expire, webhooks change, or required fields disappear.

Trigger: A scheduled workflow runs every Friday.

Action: Collect execution logs, failed jobs, retry counts, and unresolved errors. Summarize what failed and when, whether retries succeeded, which records need attention, and who owns the next action. Include references to original logs for verification.

Human checkpoint: The workflow owner reviews failures, prioritizes fixes, and assigns responsibility. Remove passwords, tokens, and unnecessary personal information before AI processing.

Measure: Unresolved failures and time to detection.

Seven automations are options, not seven mandatory launches. I recommend choosing one or two workflows and testing them properly.

Test missing information, duplicate events, failed API calls, and unexpected responses before activating customer-facing actions. Keep a manual fallback. A workflow that fails safely is more valuable than one that creates hidden mistakes.

Avoid decisions with substantial financial, legal, privacy, or customer-trust consequences. That includes:

Don't build complicated multi-agent systems when a simple trigger, rule, and notification will solve the problem.

The week-one goal isn't maximum automation. It's one reliable workflow the team understands, can measure, and can safely stop. Once it works, document failures, improve human checkpoints, and expand gradually.

Atiq Md Rezaul Hoque Turjo is Founder of NextCTL. He helps small teams ship AI automation that survives real inboxes and phone lines, starting with one workflow, clear handoffs, and no vanity dashboards.

New Fantasy Audiobook Narrated By The Voice Of ACOTAR Is A Must-Listen

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Fantasy audiobooks are beloved by readers for their appeal in the combination of an inventive world, high-stakes storytelling, and performances that make characters feel distinct. Macmillan Audio’s upcoming book release, This Wretched Alchemy by Tina Mars, brings those elements together with a notable casting connection: Jennifer Ikeda, whose work includes the first two installments of Sarah J. Maas’s A Court of Thorns and Roses series.

Arriving November 10, 2026, the audiobook pairs Ikeda with Soneela Nankani, giving Mars’s story a two-narrator presentation. The ACOTAR connection is an immediate draw for listeners familiar with Ikeda’s work, but This Wretched Alchemy's premise offers its own reasons to pay attention. This is a fantasy story built around a deadly bargain, a high-risk tournament, and an assassination mission. Its casting gives the release a recognizable link to a major fantasy series.

This Wretched Alchemy's audiobook is available to purchase now through this link

At the center of the romantasy book This Wretched Alchemy is Revel, whose family’s freedom depends on her entering a lethal contest. The tournament gathers the heirs of warring noble houses, turning their rivalries into a dangerous proving ground. Revel’s objective is not simply to survive the competition: she must use it as a route to a specific target.

That target is the Warbringer, a feared alchemist whose reputation makes him sound like a monster worth eliminating. Revel enters the contest with a clear assignment to assassinate him and secure her family’s release. The arrangement gives the story an immediate source of tension, since failure is not an abstract possibility. Her own life is at stake, while the people she hopes to save remain bound to the outcome.

The setup gives the tournament a sharper purpose than a straightforward test of skill. Each noble heir represents a competing power, and Revel must navigate their rivalries while keeping her mission concealed. Her predicament puts personal loyalty beside political maneuvering, making every alliance potentially dangerous. The contest is both a route toward freedom and a trap that could close around her.

The audiobook’s casting gives the November release an added point of interest. Jennifer Ikeda joins Soneela Nankani as a narrator, connecting This Wretched Alchemy to listeners who know Ikeda from the first two A Court of Thorns and Roses books. That association may make the new title especially appealing to ACOTAR audiobook fans looking for another fantasy world to explore. The pairing gives Mars’s cast a two-performer presentation rather than a single-voice reading. Nankani and Ikeda’s shared narration is a defining feature of Macmillan Audio’s edition.

The supposed "worst" book in the A Court of Thorns and Roses series by Sarah J. Maas has been the series' most read entry in 2026, according to data.

Beyond the casting, the story’s shift from assassination plot to complicated personal entanglement gives the performance plenty to work with. Revel’s understanding of the Warbringer begins to change when she discovers that he is more complicated than the terrifying figure she expected. What follows draws her into divine power, political betrayal, and dangerous attraction, with death still awaiting failure. Her shifting perspective complicates the mission at its heart in this must-listen new romantasy book and audiobook release..

Feng Office: a small title sample with a broad document-permission question

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Feng Office is a collaboration and document-management platform. Its data model includes companies, users, groups, projects, documents, contacts, and administrative settings. A public deployment may show only a login page, but the account behind that page can expose files and business records across an organization.

ZoomEye count-only measurements on 2026-11-09 between 23:46 and 23:47 UTC returned 12 assets for title="Feng Office", 325 for the product name in the HTTP body, and zero for app="Feng Office". The query used sub_type=all, page 1, and page size 1. This is a small title sample, so aggregate statements about a market or a vulnerability population are not justified.

The body count is larger because the name can appear in documentation, support pages, and portals. It is not a list of 325 active Feng Office installations. The title count is a more specific discovery signal, but a title match still needs confirmation from an authorized session. The zero app count reinforces the need for manual identity checks.

Feng Office's documentation describes users, groups that act as roles, and a super administrator with broad control. The owner company is special: every user who belongs to it is a potential administrator of the installation. Groups grant permission sets, and a user can receive permissions directly or through a group.

That design creates several review questions. Is the owner company limited to the right people? Can a client company administrator see another client's documents? Does a user with security-management permission have the ability to broaden their own role? Are document folders, project permissions, and contact visibility managed as one boundary or as separate settings that can drift?

The official documentation also describes a backup function that can create a ZIP containing the database and files. An account with that authority has a concentration of data and secrets. It belongs in the privileged-access review, not in a general staff-user group.

The SDK requested one record per dork with sub_type="all", page 1, and page size 1. The title, body, and application counts are exact matched totals. No full asset records or credentials were retrieved. The public search link uses the title query because it is the most specific positive signal in this sample.

An authorized reviewer can confirm the application by opening the title result, checking the login path, and comparing the page with the documented administration interface. Do not treat a body match as proof that the host is a Feng Office server.

Start with the owner company and super administrators. Confirm that the list is current and that every account has a named owner. Remove former employees and contractor accounts, then review direct permissions as well as group membership.

Review client and company boundaries. Create a test user in one client company and attempt to read a document, contact, and project that belongs to another. Test the UI and the API or export path if one is available. The result should match the documented permission model, not only the menu configuration.

Check document storage and backup access. Only a small set of administrators should be able to download a full installation backup. Store backups in a separate protected location and rotate any database or storage credentials included in the backup.

Twelve title matches do not support a claim about the total Feng Office population or about the number of exposed documents. They do support a targeted observation: the product name is visible in public data, and the control plane deserves a permission review when an organization finds one of its own instances.

The body count should be described as a candidate set. The zero application fingerprint is a limitation of the index, not a clean bill of health. A responsible inventory uses all three signals, prioritizes the title matches, and reports the remaining uncertainty.
